    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    
        \frac{1}{n!} \sum_{\Pi} \Pi^T B\, \Pi = \frac{\operatorname{tr}(B)}{n} I +\frac{\mathbf{1}^TB\,\mathbf{1}-\operatorname{tr}(B)}{n(n-1)}(\mathbf{1}\mathbf{1}^T-I),
     
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*} 
        {\end{equation*}} 
        {\csname pseudo@equation*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    
    where $I \in \mathbb{R}^{n\times n}$ is the identity matrix.
<!-- end lemma-2 -->
<!-- proof-16: body_md -->
Let $\mathbf{1}:=(1,\ldots,1)^T\in \mathbb{R}^n$, let $\mathcal{S}_n$ be the permutation group and $i,j\in\{1,\ldots,n\}$. For any permutation matrix $\Pi$ we denote its corresponding permutation $\pi \in \mathcal{S}_n$. Notably, we have $(\Pi^T B\, \Pi)_{i,j} = B_{\pi(i),\pi(j)}$. We now evaluate the on- and off-diagonal entries separately. For $i=j$, we find
     
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*} 
        {\begin{equation*}} 
        {\csname pseudo@equation*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    
        \frac{1}{n!}\sum_\Pi (\Pi^T B\, \Pi)_{i,i}  = \frac{1}{n!}\sum_{\pi\in \mathcal{S}_n} B_{\pi(i),\pi(i)}= \frac{1}{n!} \sum_{\substack{\pi\in\mathcal{S}_n,\\\pi(i)=l}}\,\, \sum_{l=1}^n B_{l,l} = \frac{(n-1)!}{n!} \operatorname{tr}(B) = \frac{\operatorname{tr}(B)}{n},
     
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*} 
        {\end{equation*}} 
        {\csname pseudo@equation*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    
    for $i\neq j$, we obtain
     
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        {\begin{align*}} 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    
        \frac{1}{n!}\sum_\Pi (\Pi^T B\, \Pi)_{i,j}  &= \frac{1}{n!}\sum_{\pi\in \mathcal{S}_n} B_{\pi(i),\pi(j)}= \frac{1}{n!} \sum_{\substack{\pi\in\mathcal{S}_n,\\\pi(i)=l,\\\pi(j)=l'}}\,\, \sum_{\substack{l,l'=1\\ l\neq l'}}^n B_{l,l'} = \frac{(n-2)!}{n!} (\mathbf{1}^T B\, \mathbf{1} - \operatorname{tr}(B))\\[-2ex]
        &=\frac{\mathbf{1}^TB\,\mathbf{1}-\operatorname{tr}(B)}{n(n-1)}.
     
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        {\end{align*}} 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    
    Assembling, the entry-wise results in a matrix concludes the proof.
<!-- end proof-16 -->
<!-- lemma-3: body_md -->
\label{lem:emp-mean-perturb}
    Let $I$ be a finite index set with $\lvert I \rvert=n$ and let the collection $(x_i)_{i\in I}$ be real-valued. Then, for any $l \in I$,
	                                                            
	 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*} 
        {\begin{equation*}} 
        {\csname pseudo@equation*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    
		\sum_{i \in I} (x_i - \bar{x})^2 = \sum_{i \in I \setminus\{l\}} (x_i - \bar{x}_{-l})^2 + \frac{n-1}{n} (x_l - \bar{x}_{-l})^2,
	 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@equation*} 
        {\end{equation*}} 
        {\csname pseudo@equation*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@equation*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    
	where $\bar{x}:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in I} x_i$ and $\bar{x}_{-l}:=\frac{1}{n-1}\sum_{i \in I \setminus\{l\}}^n x_i$.
<!-- end lemma-3 -->
<!-- proof-17: body_md -->
By definition, $\bar{x}_{-l} = \frac{n \bar{x} - x_l}{n-1}$. Plugging this equation into the right-hand side of the statement and simplifying the resulting expression, we obtain
{\allowdisplaybreaks
	                  
     
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        {\begin{align*}} 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[0]\relax} 
    
		 \sum_{i \in I \setminus\{l\}}^n (x_i - \bar{x}_{-l})^2 &+ \frac{n-1}{n} (x_l - \bar{x}_{-l})^2\\[-2ex]
		  	&=\sum_{I \setminus\{l\}} \left((x_i-\bar{x})+\frac{x_l-\bar{x}}{n-1}\right)^2 + \frac{n}{n-1} (x_l - \bar{x})^2\\
		 &= \sum_{I \setminus\{l\}} (x_i-\bar{x})^2 + 2(x_i-\bar{x})\frac{x_l-\bar{x}}{n-1}  + \frac{(x_l - \bar{x})^2}{n-1} + \frac{n}{n-1} (x_l - \bar{x})^2\\
		 &= \sum_{I \setminus\{l\}} (x_i-\bar{x})^2 + 2\Big(n\bar{x} - x_l - (n-1)\bar{x}\Big) \frac{x_l-\bar{x}}{n-1} + \frac{n+1}{n-1} (x_l - \bar{x})^2\\
		 &= \sum_{I \setminus\{l\}} (x_i-\bar{x})^2 - \frac{2}{n-1}(x_l-\bar{x})^2+ \frac{n+1}{n-1} (x_l - \bar{x})^2\\
		                                                     
		 &= \sum_{i\in I} (x_i-\bar{x})^2.
		                
     
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        { 
    \@ifundefined{pseudo@align*} 
        {\end{align*}} 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    } 
        {\csname pseudo@align*\endcsname[1]\relax} 
    
	                
}
